This authorizes the local main merge, not a push, and it does not turn the tail non-regression target into a PASS.

## Hierarchy-only incremental `CModelSource.setup()` (2026-11-03)

**Problem.** Reordering a Part/Deformer (and Undo/Redo of that move) froze the
Editor for about 1.2 s on large projects. A native Part move reaches
`CModelSource.updateModelInstances()` twice, and each call runs
`CModelSource.setup()` followed by `CModel.reinitModelInstance_exe()`.

**Attribution (real host, 5.3.04, 763 MB fixture, EDT timers).** `setup()`
averaged 2.26 s per call; `CExtendedInterpolationExtension.setupFormStructure$default`
accounted for 20.37 s of the 20.36 s of total `setup()` time over nine calls
(3069 invocations, avg 6.6 ms, max 332 ms). Every other `setup()` sub-step — the
six source sets, texture manager, motion/alias/random-pose, visible/lock
propagation — totalled about 0.1 s over the same nine calls.
`reinitModelInstance_exe()` was 39–45 ms steady and is not the freeze.

**Mechanism.** `setup()` ends with two forced
`setupFormStructure$default(ext, false, false, 3, null)` calls, one looping over
every drawable source and one over every deformer source. The forced call skips
the extension's own per-keyform `editVersion` and binding-structure comparison,
so a hierarchy-only refresh rebuilds every extended-interpolation form even
though no form input changed. The new hook replaces exactly those two call sites
(inlined, no schema change, so retransformation is legal) with a consult that
only takes the host's public incremental `updateInterpolatedForms(false)` when:

- a native edit intent was recorded on the current thread (native `beginEdit`,
  the native undo/redo listener, or the runtime hierarchy write), and
- the extension is enabled and primed, and
- every keyform's `editVersion` still equals the version retained in the
  extension's `keyformUpdateMap`, the owner bindings still match the copy the
  extension retained at its last rebuild (same parameter, interpolation type,
  insert-point count and resulting key count), and the extended morph-target set
  reports no pending structure change.

Anything else — no intent, disabled/unprimed extension, changed version,
changed bindings, pending morph structure, an unexpected rebuild or any thrown
error — runs the unmodified forced call. Admission is the reviewed artifact
digest plus an install-time anchor proof on the artifact's own bytes (exactly
two forced call sites inside `setup()`, each preceded by
`ICONST_0 ICONST_0 ICONST_3 ACONST_NULL`); unsupported artifacts refuse the hook.
Enabled by default; `-Dturboism.optimization.extendedInterpolationIncrementalSetup=false`
opts out.

**A/B on the native Windows host.** Cubism 5.3.02 official launcher, production
agent `0.68.1-SNAPSHOT` dirty build, same 763 MB fixture as PERF-052, EDT
microbenchmark of the real `CModelSource`/`CModel` instances, ZGC. "Native" legs
clear the edit intent (patched class, no intent, so the forced call runs exactly
as before); "fast" legs record intent as the runtime does before a hierarchy
write. Times are single calls in ms.

| Call | Native | Fast (incremental) |
|---|---:|---:|
| `CModelSource.setup()` | 1379 / 1381 | 268 / 248 / 241 |
| `CModelSource.updateModelInstances()` | 1366 / 1326 | 295 / 286 |
| reorder (`addChild` + `updateModelInstances`) | 1366 / 1326 | 292 / 294 |

That is about **-81 %** on `setup()` and **-78 %** on the whole model refresh,
and a hierarchy move drops from ~1.4 s to ~0.29 s on this fixture.

**Correctness evidence.** The deterministic 256×256 thumbnail of the model
(`getThumbnail(256, null)` → `BufferedImage` digest) is byte-identical between
the native and fast legs (`47e48746fd41ec27`, 256×256). `unexpectedRebuilds=0`
and `failures=0`; the host's own incremental call never reported a needed
rebuild when the retained-state comparison said nothing changed. Fallbacks were
all explained per consult: no intent on native/non-edit refreshes, extensions
with extended interpolation disabled, and real state changes (5 form-version, 70
binding, 105 morph-target mismatches over the run). The reorder leg restored the
original child order (`ROOT_CHILDREN 4`). The same hook installs and weaves
`sites=2` on 5.3.04; 5.1/5.2.03/5.3.04 carry byte-identical `setup()` anchors
(BCI 231/391).

**Limitations.** One model, one host version for the timed A/B (install-time
weaving confirmed on 5.3.04; anchor shape checked against the installed 5.1,
5.2.03 and 5.3.04 jars). The timed reorder leg records the intent explicitly
through the same bridge entry the runtime uses; host-observed intent (the
native `beginEdit` hook and the undo listener) is wired but was not exercised
through a scripted palette drag. Calling `setup()` directly is not a user edit,
so the frozen-time figure for a real palette move additionally includes the
second `updateModelInstances()` call and the palette's own work.

## Render backend hotspot attribution and native-Windows deferred GL error check (2026-11-04)

**Question.** After the render-backend branch landed, which per-frame paths still
limit deformer/vertex-move smoothness? The workload is the 763 MB
the 763MB desktop fixture, named here only by its SHA-256, on native Windows (Cubism 5.3.04 bundled JRE
17.0.3.1, Intel UHD Graphics 770), with every current optimization enabled.

**Method.** Two 5-second deformation JFR windows (QUICK workload) were recorded
with `jcmd JFR.start settings=profile` timed by the perfbench phase markers:
A = deferred GL error check off (the shipped native-Windows default), B = the
same run with `-Dturboism.optimization.mesaGlThread=true` (installs
`TURBOISM_DEFERRED_GL_ERROR_CHECK deferred=ACTIVE`, no Mesa threading on
Windows). Samples below are `jdk.ExecutionSample` + `jdk.NativeMethodSample`
on `AWT-EventQueue-0` only; a native leaf can be a blocked GL query, so these
are per-thread wall samples, not CPU time.

| Deform-phase EDT leaf | A off (110 samples / 16 frames) | B deferred (178 samples / 27 frames) |
|---|---:|---:|
| `GL4bcImpl.dispatch_glGetError1` | **53** | **0** |
| `GL4bcImpl.dispatch_glBufferSubData1` | 0 (masked by the query) | 19 |
| `warp.o.a` (warp deformer transform) | 7 | 18 |
| `GL4bcImpl.dispatch_glReadPixels1` (`GLJPanel` readback) | 7 | 16 |
| `CEViewContext.onInputEvent_exe` | 5 | 11 |
| `KeyFormMorphTargetSet.getMorphTargetList` (parameter palette) | 2 | 9 |
| `GEntity.getRenderer` / `Entities$TraverseIterable` / `HashMap` | 2 / 2 / 1 | 7 / 4 / 7 |
| `AGRenderer.compareTo` (sort) | 1 | 4 |
| `GBounds.<init>` / `Arrays.copyOf` | 0 / 0 | 4 / 4 |

The per-draw `shader/A.a → glGetError` query dominates the unmodified
deformation frame: 48 % of the EDT render samples and roughly 3.3 samples per
rendered frame. With the deferred frame-end check the query disappears from the
samples; the remaining time spreads over the line-overlay upload, the warp
transform, the JOGL panel readback, model update, palette repaint and sorting.

**Throughput A/B (MEDIUM: 100 wheel pairs, 4×12 parameters, 15 s deformation;
two interleaved runs per arm).** FPS, raw runs and medians:

| Phase | A off | B deferred | Median gain |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| wheel | 3.07 / 5.16 | 8.76 / 8.92 | 4.12 → 8.84 (+115 %) |
| parameter | 2.83 / 4.88 | 4.77 / 6.13 | 3.86 → 5.45 (+41 %) |
| deformation | 2.66 / 3.82 | 6.46 / 6.64 | 3.24 → 6.55 (+102 %) |

Median deformation frame time falls from 318 ms to 153 ms (2.08×); wheel from
260 ms to 113 ms (2.30×); parameter from 279 ms to 187 ms (1.50×). The QUICK
runs show the same direction (deform 2.85–4.58 → 5.30–8.84 FPS). CPU use is
similar or lower per frame (roughly 170–207 % of one core in B versus
181–213 % in A), so the gain is removed synchronization, not less Java work.

**Compatibility and admission.** A new exact-artifact test
(`DeferredGlErrorCheckArtifactTest`) feeds every reviewed official JAR's own
error-helper and frame-boundary bytes to the transformer: all 20 stable
versions 5.0.00–5.3.04 rewrite both classes and keep the transform admitted.
A real-host smoke then launched every one of the 20 installed stable versions
with `launcher.mesaGlThread=true`: each run wrote `status=PASS` and logged
`TURBOISM_DEFERRED_GL_ERROR_CHECK deferred=ACTIVE`. The same admission works
through persisted `config.json` (`"launcher": {"mesaGlThread": true}`) without a
JVM override, verified on 5.3.04; the managed native-Windows launcher does not
export `mesa_glthread`, so only the deferred check is installed there.

**Remaining hotspots after the deferred check.** (1) `GLineRenderer` overlay
buffers: `updateMeshData()` marks positions, colors and indices dirty together,
and every frame the render path re-uploads all three via `glBufferSubData`
(18 of 19 upload samples); colors/indices are usually unchanged, so a split
dirty/partial-update experiment is the next deformation-specific candidate.
(2) `warp.o.a` is the real deformer transform (~10 %); earlier pure-Java fast
paths were slower, so no retry without a new algorithm. (3) `GLJPanel`
`OffscreenBackend.postGL` `glReadPixels` (~9 %) is the fixed Swing/JOGL
readback. (4) The new clip-mask scissor bounds scan appeared in only 3 of 312
parameter-phase samples and did not reach the deformation top list. (5) The
`matrixScratch` transform still executes `Boolean.getBoolean` plus a
`System.getProperties()` lookup on every `GTransform.getLocalToWorldMatrix`
call; a static installer-owned field would remove two global lookups per
drawable per frame.

**Decision.** The project keeps rendering optimizations opt-in on native
Windows, so the default stays off; the settings label/tooltip now state that
native Windows installs the deferred frame-end check (Mesa threading itself is
still Linux/Proton-only). Users who need smooth deformer/vertex interaction on
Windows should enable `launcher.mesaGlThread`; the measured deformation gain is
about 2× on this fixture and host.

**Follow-up inspection: `GLineRenderer` uploads and `warp.o.a` (2026-11-04).**

`GLineRenderer.updateMeshData()` is a three-line method that marks the
positions, colors and indices buffers dirty. The deformation callers do not
change all three:

- `GSimpleLinesEntity` calls `setIndices` only when the line/topology count
  changed, then always `setPositions` and `positionsBuffer.l()`; it never sets
  colors in that path.
- `WarpDeformer_Draw` has the same split: the topology branch rebuilds indices
  and calls `updateMeshData`, while the position-only branch calls
  `setPositions` and `updateMeshData`; the colors array is not touched by the
  per-frame path.
- Deployment always calls `setup()`, which copies positions, colors and indices
  into their direct buffers, but only a dirty wrapper reaches
  `glBufferSubData`.

So each deformation update re-uploads colors and indices even though only
positions changed. The measured upload samples are 18 `GLineRenderer`
`glBufferSubData` calls in the 5 s deformation window (one sample from a real
`GMeshRenderer`). A dirty split — positions always, colors/indices only after
their source array content changed — would remove roughly two thirds of those
uploads (~6–7 % of EDT samples). The safe design is a content check against the
last marked arrays (or setter hooks if in-place mutation is ruled out), not a
blanket removal: selection colors and topology edits must still reach the GPU.
`uploadElision` cannot cover this today because the production hook is refused
when `uniformLocationCache` is enabled (the uniform lifecycle invalidates
upload content at every render boundary), and it only considers skipped frames.

`warp.o.a` is a ~3 KB pure-float routine (bilinear/affine warp projection) with
no object allocation in the reviewed 5.3.04 body. Its samples are the actual
per-vertex transform for deformers whose forms changed; incremental update
already leaves unchanged deformers native/clean, so the remaining ~10 % is the
required changed-set work. The historical pure-Java rewrite was slower (P07),
and there is no safe skip without changing the deformation result. The
remaining lever is reducing the changed-deformer set (incremental-update
coverage), not the math itself.

**Line-renderer split implementation (2026-11-04).** The opt-in
`GLineRenderer.updateMeshData()` entry guard is now implemented under the
existing upload-elision preference (it no longer shares the skipped-frame mesh
elision's uniform-cache refusal). It marks positions unconditionally and marks
colors/indices only when their source arrays differ from the last marked
content. On the 5.3.04 763 MB fixture a QUICK run recorded:

| Counter | Value |
|---|---:|
| `calls` | 6,098 |
| `colorMarks` / `colorSkips` | 2 / 6,095 |
| `indexMarks` / `indexSkips` | 3,037 / 3,060 |
| `indexElements` | 129,966 |
| `failures` | 0 |

The empty/default colors array is now recognized and never dirtied; almost all
remaining marks are the first sighting of each renderer's index array. The
actual GL upload samples are still dominated by positions
(`GLineRenderer.render → bindFloatBuffer`), which must change during
deformation, so the FPS delta on this fixture is within the noise band. The
hook is a low-risk reduction of redundant index dirty/upload calls for
color/index-bearing overlays; it is not a fix for the dominant position upload.

**`warp.o.a` algorithm check.** The native routine is a single ~3 KB,
allocation-free float loop with an inside-grid interpolation branch and an
outside-grid extrapolation branch; there is no per-vertex object or collection
allocation to remove. The incremental-update counters on the same QUICK run
were `meshDeformed=64,119` versus `meshSkipped=1,221` across 54 epochs (about
1.9 % skipped), so nearly every ArtMesh is in the changed set and the warp math
is required work, not a duplicate. A faster implementation would have to come
from outside the loop: a deformer-level dirty gate that skips
`CWarpDeformer.transformDeformer_testImpl` when both the local form and the
parent-to-canvas transform are unchanged, or a parallel/GPU rewrite. The gate
was subsequently prototyped and measured; it skips ~94 % of the calls but the
whole deformer-transform body is only 3.7 % of the deform-phase wall time, so
it is not worth productising (see the section below). The parallel/GPU rewrite
needs a new concurrency or rendering architecture and was not attempted after
P07's pure-Java rewrite measured slower.

**Deformer-level dirty gate exploration (2026-11-04).** The earlier
`transformDeformer_testImpl` gate idea was prototyped with a throw-away agent
(`build/deformer-gate-diag`, not shipped) that instruments
`CWarpDeformer.transformDeformer_testImpl` at entry and before its `RETURN`.
It compares every input of the pure transform with the committed snapshot:
child `preDeformForm` identity, positions, opacity/multiply/screen colours,
source/instance identity; parent `getCalculatedForm()` identity and warp-grid
positions; the output `deformedForm` identity, positions and style; and
`preDeformForm.isCompatible(deformedForm)`. Any doubt or exception runs native.

The redundant fraction is real: on the QUICK fixture the count-only run saw
`wouldSkip=38,239 / 42,516` calls (89.9 %), with `outMutated=0`,
`incompatible=0` and only the expected 746 first-call misses where the host
creates `deformedForm` inside the method. The skip run skipped
`34,041 / 38,786` calls (87.8 %): `param` 21,404 / 23,126 (92.6 %) and `deform`
12,637 / 13,428 (94.1 %). The gate is mechanically safe on this sample.

It is, however, not worth shipping, because the whole native transform is a
small part of the frame:

| QUICK phase | native `transformDeformer_testImpl` time | phase wall | share |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| `param` | 346.7 ms | 6,300 ms | 5.5 % |
| `deform` | 184.8 ms | 5,050 ms | 3.7 % |

The JFR phase profile agrees. In `deform`, 125 EDT execution samples were
collected; `warp.o.a` was the leaf in 18 (14.4 %), of which 16 called through
`CWarpDeformer.transformDeformer_testImpl` and only 2 through an ArtMesh. But
EDT itself was busy for only about 1.25 s of the 5.05 s wall: 80/125 samples
were under Swing repaint and 66 under `SGFramework.render3d`. Even removing
every deformer transform leaves a hard ceiling of roughly 3–5 % wall time.

The A/B FPS runs confirm that the effect is below machine noise. One pair had
the skip run faster (`wheel` 6.39 → 11.90 FPS, `param` 4.62 → 8.80 FPS), the
next pair had the count run faster (`wheel` 12.74 → 8.49 FPS, `param` 8.64 →
6.30 FPS). The conclusion is to leave `transformDeformer_testImpl` alone: the
94 % redundancy is cheap work, and a production hook would add snapshot state
and admission risk for no demonstrable frame-time gain.

The apparent EDT allocation drop in the skip runs is not a memory saving from
the gate. JFR allocation samples attribute **no allocation** to
`transformDeformer_testImpl` (or to the ArtMesh deform path) in either phase;
the transform is allocation-free. `edtAllocatedBytes` is cumulative churn, the
runs processed different event/frame counts, and the diagnostic agent itself
allocates `Pending` snapshots and array clones only on non-skipped calls.
`heapUsedEnd` and process working set showed no consistent reduction: the
`deform` heap was 3.50 GB in the count run versus 3.32 GB in the skip run, and
one pair's median working set moved in the opposite direction. The real
allocation churn is in the render path: `GMatrix44.<init>` through
`GTransform.getLocalToParentMatrix`/`getLocalToWorldMatrix` (~28 % of sampled
allocation weight in `deform`), `Arrays.copyOf` (~17 %), `ArrayList` iterators,
`Float.valueOf` and morph-target lookup.

If content-equality reuse is revisited, the higher-leverage site is the
ArtMesh deform call (`CArtMeshForm.transform` / the incremental bridge's
`deformArtMesh`), where the changed-set decision still marks nearly every mesh
dirty in editing mode; that needs its own timing experiment first. The larger
remaining frame cost is the render path itself (Swing repaint → `render3d`,
`glReadPixels`, scene traversal and line uploads), not the model transform.

## Canvas readback pipeline, MSAA and the frame budget (2026-11-04)

**Frame budget correction.** The JFR recordings use `jdk.ExecutionSample` at 10 ms and
`jdk.NativeMethodSample` at 20 ms (confirmed from `jdk.ActiveSetting` and the inter-sample
histogram). Weighted per type, the EDT is active for only 4 % of a wheel frame (5 ms of 133 ms),
34 % of a parameter frame and 42 % of a deformation frame (50 ms of 119 ms). Camera panning is
therefore paced outside the EDT, and Java-side render work has a much lower ceiling than the
earlier 20 ms-everywhere estimate implied.

**Canvas size is a measurement variable.** The GLJPanel is 830x653 when created and 1513x1253
once the window is maximised — a 3.4x pixel difference. The perf plugin now records
`<phase>.canvasWidth` / `<phase>.canvasHeight`; A/B arms must be compared only when those match.

**4x MSAA is hard-coded.** `graphics3d.shader.A.<clinit>` sets `e = 4` and `f = true`, and
`c()` passes them to `setNumSamples` / `setSampleBuffers`; there is no host setting. A throw-away
agent rewrote the two field reads to `ICONST_0` (verified `numSamples=0 sampleBuffers=0`) and ran
A/B/B/A: wheel 12.17/12.20 → 12.63/12.57 (+3.4 %), parameter +1.6 %, deformation within noise.
**Rejected** — the canvas is not fill-rate bound, so the quality cost is not worth 3 %.

**Readback ceiling.** Replacing `GLJPanel$OffscreenBackend.postGL` with a counter and an immediate
return (stale image by design) gave deform 8.88/9.11 → 10.34/10.43 (+15 %) in two pairs. The
readback itself transfers only ~2.2 MB per frame (0.5 Mpx), so the gain is the removed GPU sync,
not bandwidth. `-Dsun.java2d.opengl=true` does not help: JOGL still selects `OffscreenBackend`
(`oglPipelineUsable()` false) and the run is slower.

**Pipelined readback (shipped, opt-in).** `launcher.asyncReadback` rewrites the single GLSL-path
`GL.glReadPixels(IIIIIILjava/nio/Buffer;)V` inside `postGL` into `AsyncReadbackBridge.read`, which
queues frame N into a pixel-pack buffer and delivers frame N-1 from the other one. Only the pinned
JOGL artifact is admitted, the whole `postGL` instruction shape is re-verified per transform, and
the first pipelined delivery is compared int-by-int against the primed synchronous frame (one
mismatch disables the path permanently). Measured QUICK, same 1513x1253 canvas, adjacent arms:
wheel 12.34/12.44 → 15.13/15.24 (**+22.5 %**), parameter 8.38/8.46 → 9.56/9.71 (**+14 %**),
deformation 9.13 → 10.65 (+16.6 %); self-check mismatches 0. The canvas paints one frame late, so
the option is default-off and states the tradeoff in the settings tooltip.

**Cross-version note.** The earlier "5.3 is 35 % slower than 5.2" comparison used the 10 MB model
in `deferred2-*`; it does not apply to the 763 MB fixture (178 vs 46–48 deformation frames per 5 s
window). Do not compare frame rates across different fixtures.

**Adapter cost.** The `matrixScratch` admission gate costs 10–12 ns per call across 66k–80k calls
per frame (0.5–2.5 ms/frame) and, for the 2–3 level transform chains this fixture uses, saves no
allocations at all: both scratch slots are per-invocation locals. The telemetry sampler was also
calling `getProcessCpuLoad()` once per second, which measured **67 ms of CPU per call** on Windows
(PDH); it now latches to a `getProcessCpuTime()` delta after one probe.

**Readback hook version sweep.** Every installed build on the reviewed host shares one JOGL
artifact: 38 installations (20 stable 5.0.00–5.3.04 plus 18 betas up to 5.3.05 beta1) all carry
the same 3,616,948-byte `jogl-all.jar` (`7dbedb4b…`, the reviewed `CUBISM_5_3_03_JOGL` digest), and
every checked Editor build ships the same fourteen `graphics3d/view/glWrapper` classes and
constructs `GLJPanel`. The artifact test — digest, exact `postGL` shape, single rewritten readback,
drift refusal — passes against 5.0.00, 5.1.03, 5.2.03, 5.3.04 and 5.3.05 beta1. A real 5.2.03
launch reported `TURBOISM_ASYNC_READBACK pipelined=ACTIVE` with zero self-check failures (that
build's perfbench plugin does not produce results, so the frame-rate numbers above remain the
5.3.04 measurement).

## Windows 5.3.04 With the 763MB Cornflower Model: A Host Screening of Curated Combinations (2026-11-05)

Native Windows 5.3.04 with bundled Java 17.0.3.1 and Intel UHD 770. Each wheel leg runs 200 events in the order OFF, ON, ON, OFF with exact camera restoration and a per-pixel comparison of the model viewport for `x>=500`; the final combination really takes effect through `config.json`. The model SHA-256 is `83326ac5…b6da`.

- **Tier 1, recommended**: modelUpdateSkip, uniformLocationCache, mesaGlThread, uploadElision, meshSetupElision, clipMaskDeduplication, clipMaskScissorClear, and extendedInterpolationIncrementalSetup. The ON legs of the complete round average **231.2 and 229.0 down to 73.0 and 71.6ms**, about 3.2 times faster, and the adjacent calibration round gives about 2.3 times. The model viewport shows zero pixel difference from the baseline, and the whole-window hash of the Tier 1 calibration round also matches the baseline.
- **Attribution**: the uniform cache with the frame-end deferred GL check gives about 1.9 times, adding upload and meshSetup reaches about 2.3 times, and the clipMask scissor saves about 96.7% of the mask clear pixels on this model. The uniform cache and the frame-end deferred check must be enabled together; the uniform cache alone gives no stable gain, and mesaGlThread alone is INACTIVE on this host.
- **The edit combination**: Tier 1 plus incrementalUpdate and incrementalUpdateAffecters takes about 103.5ms on a pure camera wheel zoom, which is slower than Tier 1, so it does not enter the default and an edit or hierarchy scenario is evaluated separately.
- **Rejected or optional**: the all-enabled combination shows a 43,582-pixel difference in the model viewport and is not faster, and `warpPositionProjection` fails to install on this host; meshInterpolation, shaderUniform, and matrixScratch are visually consistent when stacked alone but slower; asyncReadback carries `oneFrameLatency=true` and gives no stable extra gain this round, so it is no strict visual-consistency default; and the old allocation and texture caches give no primary-metric gain.
- **Limits**: one model and one version; wheel is not physical FPS; the host load fluctuates between the complete and calibration rounds; and the artmesh drag probe was blocked this round by a one-pixel canvas identity check, so it produced no new drag or undo conclusion. The evidence and configuration are in [windows-cornflower-performance-20261105](../../validation/windows-cornflower-performance-20261105/README.md).


## Direct-Raster Texture Preparation: Controlled Loading on Native Windows (2026-11-06 UTC)

- **Change**: `CanonicalRgbaImage` gains a direct raster read for standard ARGB and ABGR while keeping the getRGB fallback, the size caps, and the default-off policy. The old and new product agent ZIPs differ only in the content of that class.
- **Environment**: native Windows 11, Ryzen 9 7940H, 32GB of RAM, a measured OpenGL renderer of NVIDIA RTX 4060 Laptop, and Cubism 5.3.04 with bundled Java 17.0.3.1. The model is 763,166,356 B with SHA-256 `83326ac519dd5e4cad9b39e4c7234410cc62bf0d1f5241364561d6906292b6da`, holding 1029 model images in 12 atlases.
- **Equivalence and lifecycle**: `shadow-s5` compares all 9 preparations over 402,653,184 B with zero difference in pixels, metadata, and effective row stride, and the disable, re-enable, and bytecode restore pass. Two 8192² images exceed the area cap and fall back; every observed input is ARGB, so there is no host ABGR speedup evidence.
- **The host matrix**: three groups of three rounds in the order native, legacy, direct, then direct, native, legacy, then legacy, direct, native, with no concurrent build and a fresh JVM, home, and model copy each round. All 9 rounds PASS with a normal exit of 0, Job Object `cleanup=safe`, and unchanged model and artifact hashes.
- **Result**: the old-to-new preparation callback median goes **684.739 → 389.191ms, −43.2%, saving about 296ms**, while the whole document-ready time goes 43.112 → 43.122 seconds and the CPU 36.625 → 36.844 seconds, so there is no evidence of a whole-load speedup. The native close median is 44.925 seconds, and the about 4% native-to-direct difference already existed in the old implementation, so it cannot be attributed to this fast path.
- **Unfavorable metrics kept**: the old-to-new loading working-set peak median goes **5.615 → 5.990GiB**, the commit peak **7.598 → 10.063GiB**, and the idle-tail working set **4.898 → 6.190GiB**; the inter-round fluctuation is clear, so this is attributed to no certain mechanism and no RAM saving is claimed. The observed allocation of **20.902 → 20.856GB** falls only slightly.
- **Failures kept**: the probe failed to package a shared timing helper class, which was fixed red first and green after with an added independent class-load check in the packaging; the s1 quote, s2 log recursion, s3 old field, and s4 double close wrapper failures all stay as failures. The s2 forced exit still records cleanup unknown and continues only after the original Job Object kernel shows activeProcesses=0, and it is never rewritten as a normal success.
- **Conclusion and retry condition**: the feature stays opt-in and is not recommended as a default. It covers one model and version, a non-cold disk, a 500ms readiness poll, and a 30-second input-free idle, with no interactive FPS, VRAM, video memory, or stable memory gain. The Java2D tracking risk of a direct array access is not excluded by an interactive render test. The new workload already provides evidence of two real large-image fallbacks, but widening the area to 8192² needs its own 256MiB target-buffer memory experiment, and the threshold is never widened silently.
- **Evidence**: the [complete report with the nine-round CSV and hash receipts](../../testing/host-validation/image-archive/benchmark/NATIVE-WINDOWS.md); the 14 original attempts, the failures, the JSONL and CSV, the load properties, the logs, and the source at measurement time stay in the ignored directory `build/texture-host/` of this worktree, and no user model, license state, or machine path is committed.

## Remaining Loading Hotspots: Native Windows JFR Diagnostics (2026-11-07 UTC)

- **Scope**: two further isolated JFR recordings, `profile-direct` and `profile-native`, use the profile configuration with a 96-frame stack depth and both exit normally with 0, cleanup safe, and unchanged model and artifact hashes. Only the loading trace from its start to the ready marker is analyzed, excluding the 30-second idle; `--profile` writes an envelope and the performance summarizer rejects that kind of sample, so it never mixes into the nine-round medians above.
- **The direct path still holds hotspots**: of 880 Java execution samples, the JOGL `createFromCustom` to `Graphics2D.drawImage` to `ComponentColorModel.getDataElements` path takes **194, or 22.0%**; extended interpolation initialization takes **152, or 17.3%**; model deserialization takes **130, or 14.8%**; and float parsing takes **63, or 7.2%**. These are Java sample shares, not total load time, exact CPU seconds, or an achievable speedup ratio.
- **Allocation attribution as estimated weight, not resident memory or a saveable RAM figure**: JOGL custom is about 4.73GB, the whole extended interpolation about 3.63GB, model deserialization about 4.58GB, and float parsing about 1.38GB. The bytecode that rebuilds the point reference list on every `getAllPointRef` call is verified, but the old narrow projection of P01 gives no net gain, so the same experiment is not reopened on that basis.
- **A new concrete clue**: the 193 custom texture Java samples concentrate between 35 and 40 seconds, consistent with the existing fallback of the two 8192² images, so the next candidate should be an independent large-image range or 256MiB target-buffer experiment rather than more tuning of the nine preparations already covered. No per-call size event was added, so the whole sampling weight is not attributed exactly to those two images.
- **ImageIO and archive**: PNG shows 24 Java and 50 native method samples, the stack really shows a ByteArrayInputStream source that still goes through the temporary file read, write, and seek of FileCacheImageInputStream, and the archive closure shows a whole `readBytes` with a BAOS growth copy. The candidate is a bounded in-memory image stream at the exact call site with fewer intermediate copies, never a global ImageIO cache change, never a removal of the XML or archive validation, and never a cache of a mutable form reference.
- **Anti-misreading**: the 1509 native event and network wait samples are no CPU hotspot, and the real GCPhasePause is about 0.692 seconds, so a `GarbageCollection.duration` that includes concurrent phases is never passed off as stop-the-world time. Licensing, checksums, and the GL lifecycle are never bypassed.
- **Evidence**: the [hotspot report with the classification table, the source, and the JFR hashes](../../testing/host-validation/image-archive/benchmark/HOTSPOTS.md). The raw JFR, the logs, and the local javap dumps are in the ignored directory `build/texture-host/`; a JFR may hold environment and system properties, so it is neither committed nor uploaded. This round adds no production optimization and no performance gain conclusion.

## Five Independent Hotspot Experiments: Native Windows Screening (2026-11-07 UTC)

- **Design**: a private experimental agent only, changing no product default and no production area cap. The large-image range, the PNG memory stream, the archive readAllBytes, the direct-typed read-only warp projection, and the uncached short float literal are each measured alone and never stacked. The control carries the same observation and summary scaffolding, and float uses a new probe and an independent control. Each round uses a fresh JVM, home, and model copy on a new disk with enough space, and the group is compared within itself, never mixed directly with the C-drive data of the previous section.
- **The large-image range is the most worth advancing**: 11 of 11 preparations over 939,524,096 B compare with zero shadow difference; in two bracketed control groups the ready median goes **40.774 → 39.005s, −4.3%**, and **42.877 → 41.264s, −3.8%**, while the observed allocation goes **21.058 → 16.789GB** and **21.095 → 16.751GB**, both about −20%. Each arm holds only 2 runs, so these remain screening point estimates and no stable or statistically significant claim is made. The peak working set moves 7.025 → 4.973 and 6.408 → 6.201GiB with an unstable magnitude, and the tail working set falls about 21% in both groups, which is not the same as a general RAM or video-memory gain.
- **PNG**: 11 real images agree row by row in pixels, color model, and type; the local ImageIO time goes **4.376 → 2.526s, −42.3%**, but the whole load goes **40.236 → 42.030s, +4.5%**, so merging is not recommended. Only the per-read MemoryCache is changed, never the global ImageIO, and the public InputStream mark and ownership contract still needs a narrower boundary.
- **Archive**: 2 runs over 124,757,880 B produce the same output digest, and the ZIP current-entry EOF and truncation error regressions pass. The local time goes **0.595 → 0.551s** and the whole load **41.641 → 41.178s**, which is a marginal change with no stable gain.
- **Warp**: the old per-vector MethodHandle and global callback approach is avoided and the old plan is not reopened; 5390 calls over 3,771,822 points agree on raw bits, the second write loop, the dirty flag, and the interpolation math stay, and setup is not skipped. The whole load goes **43.048 → 43.759s** with an allocation change below −1% and unfavorable peak and commit fluctuations, so it is not recommended.
- **Float**: only `d`, `-d`, `d.0`, and `-d.0` with digits 0 to 9 are recognized, negative zero is preserved, and everything else goes to the JDK. More than 50000 offline bit and exception comparisons pass, and the real 6414 hits agree with the per-token shadow; **of 6,860,804 calls only 6414 hit, 0.0935%**. The whole load goes 42.378 → 41.380 seconds, but the allocation does not fall and the coverage is extremely low, so no speedup is concluded, no special case is widened, and the old token cache is not reopened.
- **Correctness and failures**: all 26 accepted attempts, including shadow and precheck runs rather than 26 timed samples, exit normally with 0, cleanup safe, an identical archive and 256px model thumbnail digest, and unchanged input, copy, and artifact hashes, and every method of the private target is proven restored by CP-independent instructions, branches, and handlers. Three failed prechecks, control-s1, s2, and f1, stay as failures for a raw class byte comparison, a JVM non-retransformable cache, and a float Object return descriptor. The later real JVM restore and the exact-host offline weave regression pass, and no failure is rewritten as a success.
- **Limits and decision**: one model, 5.3.04, and an RTX 4060, with 2 runs per arm per group and a baseline fluctuating between 39 and 44 seconds, on a non-cold disk, with no interactive FPS, undo, or save matrix. Only the large-image range is recommended for a wider memory and compatibility verification; no candidate of this batch is merged and none is enabled by default. The local PNG and large-image gains must not be added to predict an untested combination.
- **Evidence**: the [five complete reports with the 29-attempt CSV and the independent cohort and hash receipts](../../testing/host-validation/image-archive/experiments/RESULTS.md). The private raw evidence pointer is `build/texture-experiments/run-root.private.txt`; two measurement probe versions and the runner line-ending encoding are saved by their original source SHA, and no model, license, or machine path is committed.
